class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        self.r = len(pizza)
        self.c = len(pizza[0])
        
        tot_apples = 0
        self.pfsum_row = []
        self.pfsum_col = []
        
        for i in range(self.r):
            pfr = 0
            pfs_r = [0] * self.c
            pfs_c = [0] * self.c
            for j in range(self.c):
                if i > 0:
                    pfs_c[j] += self.pfsum_col[i - 1][j]
                if pizza[i][j] == 'A':
                    pfr += 1
                    pfs_c[j] += 1
                    tot_apples += 1
                pfs_r[j] = pfr
            self.pfsum_row.append(pfs_r)
            self.pfsum_col.append(pfs_c)
        
        if tot_apples < k:
            return 0
        
        if k = 1:
            return 1
        
        return self.getWays(0, 0, k) % (1000000007)
    
    
    @cache
    def getWays(self, i, j, k):
        if k == 1:
            found = False
            for c in range(j, this.c):
                apple_in_region = this.pfsum_col[this.r - 1][c]
                if i > 0:
                     apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_col[i - 1][c]
                if apple_in_region:
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                return 1
            return 0
        else:
            cannot_cut = True
            nr = i
            t_cnt = 0
            while nr < this.r - 1:
                while nr < this.r - 1 and cannot_cut:
                    apple_in_region = this.pfsum_row[nr][this.c - 1]
                    if j > 0:
                        apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_row[nr][j - 1]
                    if apple_in_region:
                        cannot_cut = False
                    else:
                        nr += 1
                        
                if nr < this.r - 1:
                    t_cnt += this.getWays(nr + 1, j, k - 1)
                nr += 1
                
            cannot_cut = True
            nc = j
            while nc < this.c - 1:
                while nc < this.c - 1 and cannot_cut:
                    apple_in_region = this.pfsum_col[this.r - 1][nc]
                    if i > 0:
                        apple_in_region -= this.pfsum_col[i - 1][nc]
                    if apple_in_region:
                        cannot_cut = False
                    else:
                        nc += 1
                
                if nc < this.c - 1:
                    t_cnt += this.getWays(i, nc + 1, k - 1)
                nc += 1
            
            return t_cnt

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 1000000007
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])
        
        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i+1][j+1] = (pref[i][j+1] + pref[i+1][j]
                                  - pref[i][j] + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0))
        
        def has_apple(r1, c1, r2, c2):
            return pref[r2+1][c2+1] - pref[r1][c2+1] - pref[r2+1][c1] + pref[r1][c1] > 0
        
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i, j, k):
            if k == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r-1, c-1) else 0
            
            ans = 0
            for nr in range(i, r-1):
                if has_apple(i, j, nr, c-1):
                    ans += dp(nr+1, j, k-1)
            for nc in range(j, c-1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r-1, nc):
                    ans += dp(i, nc+1, k-1)
            return ans % MOD
        
        if sum(row.count('A') for row in pizza) < k:
            return 0
        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

def dp(i,j,k):

            ans += dp(i,y+1,k-1)

Let's write final code carefully:

```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 1000000007
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])

        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        def has_apple(r1: int, c1: int, r2: int, c2: int) -> bool:
            return pref[r2 + 1][c2 + 1] - pref[r1][c2 + 1] - pref[r2 + 1][c1] + pref[r1][c1] > 0

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, c - 1) else 0

            ans = 0
            for nr in range(i, r - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, nr, c - 1):
                    ans = (ans + dp(nr + 1, j, pieces - 1)) % MOD
            for nc in range(j, c - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, nc):
                    ans = (ans + dp(i, nc + 1, pieces - 1)) % MOD
            return ans

        if sum(row.count('A') for row in pizza) < k:
            return 0
        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

Let's recall standard code:
```
def dfs(r, c, k):
    if k == 0:
        return 1 if hasApple(r, c, rows-1, cols-1) else 0
    ans = 0
    for i in range(r+1, rows):
        if hasApple(r, c, i-1, cols-1):
            ans += dfs(i, c, k-1)
    for j in range(c+1, cols):
        if hasApple(r, c, rows-1, j-1):
            ans += dfs(r, j, k-1)
    return ans
```
This is exactly our dp: state (i,j) is top-left of current piece, and you cut off top/left pieces, continuing on bottom/right. That corresponds to current piece anchored at (i,j), not original (0,0), and you are cutting off pieces containing the current top-left? Let's check with known LeetCode solution code. I recall a common solution:
```
class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        m, n = len(pizza), len(pizza[0])
        pref = [[0]*(n+1) for _ in range(m+1)]
        for i in range(m-1,-1,-1):
            for j in range(n-1,-1,-1):
                pref[i][j] = pref[i+1][j] + pref[i][j+1] - pref[i+1][j+1] + (pizza[i][j]=='A')
        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i,j,k):
            if k == 0:
                return 1 if pref[i][j] > 0 else 0
            ans = 0
            for x in range(i+1,m):
                if pref[i][j] - pref[x][j] > 0:
                    ans += dp(x,j,k-1)
            for y in range(j+1,n):
                if pref[i][j] - pref[i][y] > 0:
                    ans += dp(i,y,k-1)
            return ans
        return dp(0,0,k-1)
```
Wait this uses pref[i][j] as apples in subpizza from (i,j) to bottom-right. It checks top piece by `pref[i][j] - pref[x][j] > 0` (top piece rows i..x-1, cols j..n-1) and recurses on bottom piece `dp(x,j,k-1)`. This is exactly our dp. So standard solution sets aside top/left piece and continues on bottom/right. Therefore problem statement must be "After each cut, you take the piece that does NOT contain the upper-left corner and continue cutting it"? Let's recall exact wording: "You cut the pizza into k pieces using k-1 cuts. Each cut is a straight horizontal or vertical cut across the entire pizza. After cutting the pizza, the piece on the upper-left is used for further cutting" Hmm no.

But many solutions use top-left of remaining piece? Let's inspect official solution code from memory:
```
def dp(r, c, k):
    if k == 0:
        return 1 if pref[r][c] else 0
    ans = 0
    for nr in range(r+1, rows):
        if pref[r][c] - pref[nr][c] > 0:
            ans += dp(nr, c, k-1)
    for nc in range(c+1, cols):
        if pref[r][c] - pref[r][nc] > 0:
            ans += dp(r, nc, k-1)
    return ans
```
Here `pref[r][c]` is apples in subpizza from (r,c) to bottom-right. `dp(r,c,k)` means current piece is subpizza with top-left (r,c). If you cut horizontally at nr, the top piece rows r..nr-1 is set aside, and you recurse on bottom piece top-left (nr,c). This is exactly "take the piece that does NOT contain the upper-left corner" of the current piece. The problem statement might say "After each cut, you take the piece with the upper-left corner and continue cutting it" but maybe they define the upper-left corner of the current piece? If you cut horizontally, the top piece contains the upper-left corner; if you take it and continue, you'd recurse on top. So no.

Let's search memory of problem statement from LeetCode: "You are given a pizza consisting of rows and columns. You decide to cut the pizza into k pieces using k-1 cuts. For each cut, you choose a straight horizontal or vertical line that cuts the current pizza in half, then you take the piece that has the upper-left corner and continue cutting it." Wait if you take the piece that has the upper-left corner, then after cutting horizontally, the top piece has the upper-left corner, so you continue cutting the top piece. But then the bottom piece is set aside. This means you are always cutting the top-left piece, and the current piece's bottom-right corner moves up/left. The DP state would be bottom-right corner, and you would cut off bottom/right pieces. The standard DP's state top-left of remaining piece would be reversed. Which one is official? Let's think of the name "ways of cutting pizza" and images. I recall a LeetCode problem where you cut the pizza and "After each cut, you take the piece with the upper-left corner and continue cutting it." Actually yes, I remember reading that. But the official solution? Let's search memory of code from a known blog:
```
class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        m, n = len(pizza), len(pizza[0])
        dp = [[[0]*(n+1) for _ in range(m+1)] for _ in range(k)]
        ...
```
Maybe they use bottom-up DP with `dp[k][i][j]` where `i,j` is bottom-right corner? Let's recall: There is a solution that uses prefix sum from bottom-right and `dp[i][j][k]` where `(i,j)` is bottom-right corner of the piece that has not been cut? Hmm.

Let's search memory of LeetCode 1444 discussion: Many solutions define `dp[i][j][k]` as number of ways to cut the sub-pizza with upper-left corner at `(i,j)` into `k` pieces. They compute prefix sum `pref[i][j]` as number of apples in rectangle from `(i,j)` to `(m-1,n-1)`. Then:
```
for i in range(m):
    for j in range(n):
        for k in range(1,K):
            for x in range(i+1,m):
                if pref[i][j] - pref[x][j] > 0:
                    dp[i][j][k] += dp[x][j][k-1]
```
This is definitely standard. So state is top-left of remaining piece, and you cut off top/left pieces. This corresponds to "after each cut, you take the piece that does NOT contain the upper-left corner and continue cutting it" or equivalently "you cut off a piece from the top or left of the current piece and continue with the rest." The problem statement likely says: "You cut the pizza into k pieces using k-1 cuts. Each cut is a straight horizontal or vertical cut across the entire pizza. After cutting the pizza, you take the piece with the upper-left corner and continue cutting it." The state could be bottom-right corner of the top-left piece. But standard DP cuts off top pieces from the bottom-right piece. That's the opposite. If you cut horizontally after row0, then vertically after col0 on bottom row, you get 3 pieces. To get 4, you would need to cut top row too, but top row was set aside and not cut. === RAW ANSWER ===
with code.</think>```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 1000000007
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])

        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        def has_apple(r1: int, c1: int, r2: int, c2: int) -> bool:
            return pref[r2 + 1][c2 + 1] - pref[r1][c2 + 1] - pref[r2 + 1][c1] + pref[r1][c1] > 0

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, c - 1) else 0

            ans = 0
            for nr in range(i, r - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, nr, c - 1):
                    ans = (ans + dp(nr + 1, j, pieces - 1)) % MOD

            for nc in range(j, c - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, nc):
                    ans = (ans + dp(i, nc + 1, pieces - 1)) % MOD

            return ans

        if sum(row.count('A') for row in pizza) < k:
            return 0

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def ways(self, pizza: List[str], k: int) -> int:
        MOD = 1000000007
        r = len(pizza)
        c = len(pizza[0])

        pref = [[0] * (c + 1) for _ in range(r + 1)]
        for i in range(r):
            for j in range(c):
                pref[i + 1][j + 1] = (
                    pref[i][j + 1] + pref[i + 1][j] - pref[i][j]
                    + (1 if pizza[i][j] == 'A' else 0)
                )

        def has_apple(r1: int, c1: int, r2: int, c2: int) -> bool:
            return pref[r2 + 1][c2 + 1] - pref[r1][c2 + 1] - pref[r2 + 1][c1] + pref[r1][c1] > 0

        @lru_cache(None)
        def dp(i: int, j: int, pieces: int) -> int:
            if pieces == 1:
                return 1 if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, c - 1) else 0

            ans = 0
            for nr in range(i, r - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, nr, c - 1):
                    ans = (ans + dp(nr + 1, j, pieces - 1)) % MOD

            for nc in range(j, c - 1):
                if has_apple(i, j, r - 1, nc):
                    ans = (ans + dp(i, nc + 1, pieces - 1)) % MOD

            return ans

        if sum(row.count('A') for row in pizza) < k:
            return 0

        return dp(0, 0, k) % MOD
